They're decent motors, but the emission systems on them are an absolute nightmare. \n\nHe was getting a derate code for the DEF system—diesel exhaust fluid. The computer thought the tank was empty even though he just filled it. Usually, that means the DEF header unit is shot. It's this long probe that goes down into the tank to measure the level, the temperature, and the quality of the fluid all at once. The problem is, the coolant lines run through it to keep the DEF from freezing in the winter, and if those lines leak inside the tank, it contaminates the whole system. You have to drain the tank, flush the lines, replace the header, and sometimes even swap out the dosing valve if the crystalized DEF clogged it up. It's a messy, awful job, and the fluid smells like stale ammonia. You get it on your hands and it just dries out your skin instantly...\n\nSorry. I'm doing it again. Did you ask if I was feeling down?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt down or lost interest in things outside of work, even when you’re not thinking about the foreclosure or money issues?\n\nPatient: I don't think so, no. I mean, I don't have a ton of hobbies outside of the shop anyway, but I still care about the things I normally do. Like, I've got this old Ford F-250 sitting in my driveway. It's a 7.3 Powerstroke—the good year, before they ruined them with all that extra garbage. I was actually out there messing with it this past weekend. \n\nThe high-pressure oil pump is starting to weep a little bit. You can always tell because you start smelling oil and it pools down in the valley pan of the engine block. It's a classic issue with those 7.3s. The O-rings on the HPOP lines just get brittle from all the extreme heat cycles, and eventually, they let go. If you ignore it, you lose pressure and the truck won't even start when it's hot. It's kind of a pain to fix, though. You have to pull the whole fuel bowl assembly just to reach the fittings, and it's a really tight squeeze back there against the firewall. I bought the rebuild kit a while ago, the one with the upgraded Viton seals that are supposed to handle the heat better, and it's just sitting on my workbench. I usually like doing that kind of stuff on a Sunday, just put the radio on and wrench on my own stuff for a change without a service manager breathing down my neck... \n\nWait, I lost my train of thought. What was the question again? Something about my days off?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt down or lost interest in things you normally enjoy doing on your days off, like spending time with family, hobbies, or relaxing activities?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't know if I'd say I've lost interest, but... things have definitely changed. Like, at work, they actually took me out of the high-risk repair bay. Which, I mean, I get it, I've been a little distracted with the whole house situation, but it still stings, you know? That's where all the heavy diagnostic stuff happens, the really complicated tear-downs. Like last month, we had this Caterpillar C15 come in, and the guy had somehow managed to drop a valve. The piston just hammered it into the cylinder head. It was a complete mess. You have to pull the whole head, check the liner for scoring, make sure the connecting rod isn't bent... it's a massive job. You need the overhead crane just to lift the head off because it weighs like 400 pounds. I usually love getting into those deep engine rebuilds. You have to be so precise with the torque specs and the overhead valve adjustments. If you're off by even a few thousandths of an inch on the injector height, the engine will run like garbage and blow black smoke everywhere. \n\nBut yeah, they moved me to lighter duty for now. And outside of work... I guess I just haven't been doing much. I used to volunteer at church, go to these fitness classes, and I'd always go to the truck-club outings. About my days off?\n\nDoctor: Have you found it harder to make decisions or felt like your mind goes blank, even about things that wouldn't usually bother you?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed my mind going blank or anything like that. I mean, I still make decisions fine, mostly. Like at the shop, even on light duty, you still have to decide the best way to tackle a job. Yesterday I was doing a brake job on this municipal dump truck—an International WorkStar. The rear drums on those things are massive, and the spring brakes can be really dangerous if you don't cage them right before you pull the chamber off. There's a heavy-duty coil spring inside that chamber holding back a couple thousand pounds of force, and if you just unbolt the clamp band without caging it with the T-bolt, that spring will blow the whole housing apart. People have gotten killed by it. It's crazy to think about, really. Just one little bolt is all that stands between you and a trip to the ER. You always have to make sure the threads on the T-bolt aren't stripped, too. I've seen guys try to use rusty ones they found in the bottom of their toolbox, and I always yell at them. It's just not worth the risk for a five-dollar part...\n\nSorry, I'm doing it again, aren't I? Like, I went to the grocery store the other day and I knew exactly what I needed for this chili recipe I make. It's a really good one, actually. I use a mix of ground chuck and a little bit of spicy Italian sausage, which sounds weird for chili, but it adds this incredible depth of flavor. You have to brown the meat first, obviously, but the trick is to deglaze the pan with a dark stout beer instead of beef broth. It just gives it this rich, earthy taste. And I always toast my own cumin seeds before grinding them. The pre-ground stuff in the little plastic bottles at the store is just sawdust by the time you buy it. You throw the whole seeds in a dry skillet over medium heat for just a couple of minutes until they start smelling fragrant, and then you hit them in a mortar and pestle. It makes a huge difference. Yeah, no, I think I'm fine with that kind of stuff.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difficulty thinking clearly, concentrating, or making decisions, even about small or routine things?\n\nPatient: No, I really haven't noticed any issues with my concentration or making decisions on small things. You have to keep everything in exact order or you'll lose your mind on the shop floor. I have this massive Snap-on double-bank roll cab, and I was going through my socket drawers. \n\nIt's funny, no matter how careful you are, the 10-millimeter sockets always just vanish into thin air. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, yeah, I guess I have. I mean, at work, they actually took me out of the high-risk repair bay. Which, honestly, is probably for the best right now, but it still stings a little. And outside of work... I just don't do the stuff I used to. We used to do these really cool weekend trail runs with the truck club. A lot of the guys have these heavily modified rigs—mostly older Toyota Tacomas or Jeep Cherokees, the XJ body style, you know? The ones with the 4.0-liter inline-six engine. That engine is practically bulletproof. You can run it hot, run it low on oil, and it just keeps ticking. My buddy Dave has an '98 XJ, and he put this massive long-arm suspension lift on it. We spent like three weekends in his driveway welding the brackets to the unibody because if you don't reinforce it, the torque will just rip the control arm mounts right off the frame rails. We were using this old Lincoln stick welder he bought off Craigslist for like fifty bucks, and it kept tripping the breaker in his garage. We'd weld for five minutes, the power would go out, and we'd have to walk into the house in the dark to flip the switch. His wife was getting so mad because she was trying to watch some baking show on TV... 

Wait, what was the question? Oh, losing interest in things. Yeah, I just... I haven't been doing any of that lately. I just don't have the drive for it anymore.

Well, I wouldn't say I'm down or sad most of the day, no. It's not like that. I mean, sure, I'm stressed about the house and work, but I'm not just sitting around crying for no reason. I still get up, make my coffee... I have this really great French press, actually. It's one of those double-walled stainless steel ones, not the glass kind. The glass ones always break. I swear, I went through three of them in a year. You just tap them against the edge of the sink while you're washing them, and—crack. But this stainless one, you could drop it on a concrete floor and it wouldn't even dent. It keeps the coffee hot for hours, too. I buy these whole beans from this little roaster downtown. They do this medium roast from Ethiopia that has these crazy blueberry notes. You wouldn't think coffee could taste like blueberries, but it really does. You have to grind it coarse, though, otherwise you get all this sludge at the bottom of your cup. My grinder is this old burr grinder I found at a garage sale for five bucks... 

Sorry, I'm doing it again, aren't I? Just talking and talking. What were you asking? Feeling sad all day? No, I don't really feel like that.

The DPF filters get completely choked up with soot if you just idle them or putt around town. You really have to get them up to highway speeds so the exhaust gets hot enough to run a proper regen cycle, or else you're looking at a massive repair bill and you're stuck in limp mode on the side of the highway. I mean, the track tension was all out of whack, and the sprockets were just chewed to pieces. The guy who was operating it clearly didn't know how to grease the pivot shafts. You see that a lot with these newer operators—they just climb up in the cab, turn the key, and start pushing dirt, but they never do their daily walkarounds. And with a machine that heavy, if you ignore the track sag, you're going to wear out the front idlers so fast, and those things weigh a ton and cost an absolute fortune to replace. Usually, that means there's an issue with the solenoids under the valve cover, or maybe the wiring harness got brittle from the oil heat and cracked. You have to pull the whole top end off just to test the ohms on those solenoids, and getting the gasket to seat right when you put it back together is a real pain if you don't clean the mating surface perfectly. You have to trust your ears and your hands. I started checking the fuel injectors, and sure enough, the wiring harness going to the number four injector was rubbing against the engine block. The insulation was worn right through, so every time the engine vibrated, it was shorting out against the metal. It's such a simple thing, but if you don't know where to look, you could spend days tearing the fuel system apart. I just wrapped it in some high-temp wire loom and zip-tied it out of the way. The computer thought the tank was empty even though he just filled it. Usually, that means the DEF header unit is shot. It's this long probe that goes down into the tank to measure the level, the temperature, and the quality of the fluid all at once. The problem is, the coolant lines run through it to keep the DEF from freezing in the winter, and if those lines leak inside the tank, it contaminates the whole system. You have to drain the tank, flush the lines, replace the header, and sometimes even swap out the dosing valve if the crystalized DEF clogged it up. No, not really. Like I was saying, I'm definitely stressed out, but I don't really have days where I'm just depressed the whole time. Usually I'm just too busy trying to keep my head above water at the shop. 

Like yesterday, they had me looking at this massive Caterpillar D8 dozer that got hauled into the yard. The foreman was yelling at him, but the rest of us couldn't stop laughing. It took him like an hour at the sink with the Gojo pumice soap just to get it off his neck. That Gojo stuff is absolute magic, though, the orange kind with the pumice in it. It smells like fake oranges, but it takes off diesel soot, hydraulic fluid, whatever. I actually buy it by the gallon for my own house because regular soap just doesn't cut it when you've been turning wrenches all day. You have to scrub your cuticles with this little wooden brush, otherwise you leave grease stains on your towels, and my husband hates that... 

Man, I completely lost my train of thought. I'm doing it again. You asked about feeling down, right? Yeah, no, I haven't really noticed that happening.
